...shining. After this, a remarkable ship, above which sits a naked man; beneath him, the middle of a dead woman's body. A man holds a dog-headed man <note>original: "diminut' capite canī", referring to a Cynocephalus</note> by the shoulder, which the Persian language calls *Plaz*, the Greek *Cynocephali*, and the Latin *Antipites* <note>Cynocephali were mythical dog-headed humans often described in medieval bestiaries and astronomical texts</note>. Near the Indians, a woman surrounded by curls, honorable, full of demonic threads, dressed in cloth that is partly burnt, for which she anxiously seeks threads and ornaments. After the Greeks, the middle of the Fish and the haunch and head, which he carries in his left hand; the thighs and flanks of the Ram, and the place of the Ram's infection, the bay and foam of the Eridanus or the Nile.

¶ In the second **decan** <note>A decan is a 10-degree segment of a zodiac sign, each ruled by a specific deity or spirit</note> of **Taurus**: near the Persians, a ship above which a naked man reaches toward a part of the ship with his hand raised, carrying a key; and the second half of a dead woman, half a body, carrying a spear and a point in her right hand and a key in her left, assigning with both hands. Near the Indians, a man similar in body and face to his wife, resembling a bull, pointing with his finger, imitating the head and hooves, with a robust body and a burning stomach, gluttonous and impatient of hunger, dressed in an old linen cloth, anxious to inhabit houses and lands and to yoke oxen to the plow. After these, the form of a defective body, holding a rod in the right hand and the left raised on high. After the Greeks, the knees of the Fish with the shin and the hind foot; the back of the Bull and the shoulder with the right foot; the River from the beginning almost to the ends.

¶ In the third **decan** of **Taurus**: near the Persians, the end of the two-headed one, then an upright man holding snakes; then two wagons upon which a young man sits, with two horses pulling the wagons, dragging a plow here and there with his left hand. Near the Indians, a man with very white teeth, curved lips, long feet, of a reddish color with red hair, a body composed of an elephant and a lion, of troubled senses and malevolent, sitting on a stool wrapped in a carpet, black and horrible, with whom the northern horse and the bull are prostrated. After the Greeks, the right foot of the Fish and the shoulder pulling the reins with the left hand along with the end of the bridle; the left foot, the head of the Bull, and the knees with the root of the horns; also the end under **Orion's** hand and the end of the River with its bay.

¶ The sign of **Gemini** is of a sharp nature, sweet to the taste, the color of the sky, and of upright stature. It rises in its first decan, as the ancients please:

Near the Indians, a beautiful, benevolent woman, upright and anxious, seeking threads and ornaments for sewing, learned in beautiful craftsmanship; with her rises a transparent mirror. ¶ In the second **decan** of **Gemini**: near the Persians, a man singing with a golden reed. In the Persian language called *Ternuclles*, in Greek *Hercules*; the same is "the one on his knee" <note>Engonasin, the Kneeler</note> and likewise the serpent ascending the trees, yet fleeing the *Yelloni*; the middle of the horned serpent with which a hand-marked wolf appears. Near the Indians, a man like an Ethiopian, grey in color, his head bound with a leaden fillet, bound in arms; covered with an iron helmet as they are, he runs holding a bow and arrows in his hand, preparing games and leaps, singing, striking a drum, and snatching apples from a garden; at the same time there rises with him much fragrant wood. After the Greeks, the right hand of the Charioteer and the height of the hind feet of the Bull, together with the head, shoulder, hand, and feet of Orion.

the belt of the knee with the foot of a hare: and the chest and the haunch. ¶ In the third decan <note>A decan is a 10-degree segment of the zodiac; each sign has three.</note>, according to the **Persians**, is *Aslon* <note>original: "aslon"</note>, which the **Arabs** interpret as a musical drummer; above his head is a leafy myrtle, and with him a heart and golden reeds; then a barking dog with a dolphin and a linen cloth; after these are the ornaments of a shoemaker and the first half of the Lesser Bear with the tail of a horned serpent <note>original: "cerustis"</note> embracing the root of an ear of grain. According to the **Indians**, a man seeking weapons to put on, carrying a bow and quiver, in one hand an arrow; thinking of celebrating laborious weavings and shipwrecks, musical melodies, jests, and manifold joys. According to the **Greeks**, the shoulders, hands, haunch, and foot of Pollux; and from the hip and foot of a beaver <note>original: "cricastoris"</note>, the foot of a hare, the erect tail of a dog, and the right foot and the very first part of a farmer; the oar of a ship with the end of the second.

¶ **Cancer**, being of a watery nature and salty to the taste, rises in its first decan as it appeared to the **Persians**: half of the Lesser Bear, with which perfect form—in the **Roman** tongue—is a Satyr wrapped in torn rags, near *Aslon* the musician striking a drum, and at the same time a plate of iron whose head is bronze; with the first of the three girls <note>likely referring to the Graces or specific constellations</note> among the virgins; after which is the head of a beetle <note>original: "scarabonis"</note> and the tail of an asp has been dismembered. According to the **Indians**, a young man of bright beauty, dressed in decorated old clothes, with a face and fingers somewhat twisted, his body composed of a horse and an elephant, his feet hung about with kinds of fruits and the leaves of trees; whose dwelling is in a field where scandal is born. According to the **Greeks**, the face of Callisto <note>The Great Bear</note> with both heads of the Twins and a hand; then the Lesser Dog, the remainder of the Greater, and the stern of the *Argo* <note>The mythological ship constellation</note> with the handles of the oars.

¶ In the second decan of **Cancer**, according to the **Persians**, an eloquent girl with three virgins and something like a cloud; after these the front half of a dog with half the ears of a northern donkey, the middle of a beetle, and the middle of the limbs. According to the **Indians**, a girl of peaceful countenance, wearing a crown of cedar and red myrtle on her head, carrying a wooden rod in her hand, crying out in a high voice about her love, and about pruning and singing the praises of the gods in a temple. According to the **Greeks**, the head of Callisto with the rear pincer of the Crab and the end of the stern of the *Argo*.

¶ In the third decan of **Cancer**, according to the **Persians**, the third girl of the three virgins, bringing with her one now approaching, not receding; and the rear half of a dog with the second half of the donkey's ears, and likewise the second southern donkey; then the end of the beetle and the head of both. According to the **Indians**, a man having a foot like a tortoise <note>original: "tartuce"</note>, similar to a strap or what the **Arabs** call flax-moth <note>original: "tiniam lini"</note>, dyed in color, above whose body a snake is stretched out; he himself is adorned with gold-embroidery, thinking of flying across the sea in a ship for the business of gold and silver, from which he might obtain women's ornaments. According to the **Greeks**, the back of the head of the Greater Bear with the right hand and foot, then the head of the she-donkey which we call a serpent <note>original: "colubrẽ"</note>, the sail of a ship, and so forth.

¶ **Leo**, being of a choleric fire, rises in its first decan as the **Persians** themselves write: the tail of a dog, the form of a lion lying in an arch, half a ship with an oar and a small boat, the head of the Hydra, the head of a horse, the head of a donkey. According to the **Indians**, trees of spread-out root, bearing a "thief-dog" <note>original: "canem furionem"</note> and bird-snares on their branches; and likewise a man dressed in filthy rags, appearing...

# The Decans of Leo and Virgo

...preparing to mourn, with whom the lord of the horse looks toward the northern part; seen at the same time with the dry [branch] and the arrow, the head of the dog <note>the star Sirius</note> together with the dog. Following the Greeks: the neck of the Great Bear with the left hand, the jaws of the Lion with the hand, the neck of the female donkey, and the middle of the Ship <note>the constellation Argo Navis</note>. ¶ In the **second decan of Leo**, according to the Persians: an idol with a raised hand crying out with a loud voice, with whom are dancing drums; at first likewise many-sided songs, yet an old man full of Bacchus <note>original: "plenus bacho," meaning drunk on wine</note> with a bowl and glassware, and at the same time the double-flutes of the fields. Then Anathais and Harabien and a tail, stretching out a hand. In the second half of the Ship, you discern the Hydra, the middle of the horse, and the middle of the donkey.

¶ According to the Indians: a man with a sharp nose, wearing a crown of white myrtle on his head, carrying a bow in the manner of robbers; he is hot, fierce with a lion-like ferocity, and clothed in lion-colored muslin. Following the Greeks: the shoulders of the Greater Bear with the right hand, the hollow of the Lion, and the *barmus* <note>likely a corruption of a Greek anatomical or stellar term</note>, the middle of the Serpent with the prow of the Ship. ¶ In the **third decan of Leo**, according to the Persians: a youth whose name is Seclos, driving a wagon with a great whip, leading a man sitting above; there follows Parfoz <note>a Persian star-deity or constellation name</note> with the left hand, dragging a bull and a cow; then the Crow, the middle of the Hydra, the rear of the donkey, and the rear of the horse. According to the Indians: a man in the form of an Ethiopian, at once foul, obscene, and heavy with much painful anxiety, holding both meat and an apple in his hand. Following the Greeks: the chest of the Greater Bear, the middle of the Lion, and Parfoz of the Serpent.

<image-desc>A small woodcut illustration of the zodiac sign Virgo. A woman is depicted sitting on a simple throne or bench, facing left. She wears a headdress and long robes. She holds an ear of grain (spica) in each hand.</image-desc>

¶ **Virgo** is a fertile sign, bipartite and triform. It rises in its **first decan** as the Chaldeans, Egyptians, and the masters of all, Hermes <note>Hermes Trismegistus</note> and Alcalius <note>likely Al-Khwarizmi or a similar Arabic authority</note>, have taught. From earliest age, there is a girl whose particular name is *Secundidos*—translated from the Arabic of Darça Maarabice as *Nedesa*, that is, a pure virgin girl. I say an immaculate virgin: beautiful in body, lovely in countenance, modest in habit, with long hair, holding two ears of grain in her hand, sitting upon a golden throne; nourishing a boy and feeding him by right in a place whose Hebrew name signifies a boy—I say a boy named by certain nations as *Ihelum*, signifying *Eiça* <note>Arabic: Isa</note>, whom we in Greek call **Christ**. He rises with that Virgin as if sitting on the same leaf and not touching it; and likewise the star of the Ear of Grain <note>Spica</note> which is the end of the second Serpent, then the head of the stag and the head of the lion. According to the Indians: a virgin girl clothed in muslin and ancient rags; in her hand is the appearance of butter <note>original: "vtyro," likely butyro</note>, and being caught by the hand, she stands in the midst of beautiful myrtle, wishing to go to the homes of parents and friends to seek clothing and jewels. Following the Greeks: the point of the Dragon's tail, the tail of the Bear, the shackle of the Lion's loin, the feet and tail with the Bowl <note>the constellation Crater</note> at the head of the Serpent, and part of the Serpent. ¶ In the **second decan of Virgo**, according to the Persians: a musician striking a drum and likewise playing a reed pipe; after him a man, the lord of the comet, with half a form, called *Albeçe* in the Persian tongue and *Phaeton* in the Roman; or he is a man whose head is that of a bull, and in his hand is a naked half-man; after this a wooden digging tool with an iron head, and the tail of the Hydra, half of the Crow, and half of the Lion.

¶ According to the Indians: a black, hairy man clothed in a triple cloth of leather, silk, and red linen, carrying ink in his hand, intending to calculate expenses and gains. Following the Greeks: part of the Dragon's tail, the loin of the Great Bear, the end of the oblong [star] which they call *Adde Yba*, the head of the Virgin and the left shoulder, the head of the Crow with its beak and...

♎ **Libra**, a temperate sign brought forth at its rising: of two colors in form, of upright stature. It rises in its **first decan** <note>A decan is a 10-degree segment of a zodiac sign, each ruled by different planetary influences.</note> as the Persians assert: an angry man holding a balance scale in his left hand; in his right a lamb with which books are inscribed; and a third part of his knowledge, the names of which are *Carathimeme*. In their path is a musician sitting on a horse, striking a drum and playing a reed pipe; after this the head of the Dragon and its first part, which the Persians call the Greater Bear, with a white ship. According to the Indians, a man carrying a measure <note>term: modius - a dry measure for grain</note> and a balance scale to the market, sitting in tents, learnedly intending to weigh and trade in his mind. According to the Greeks, the middle of the tail of the Greater Bear; and the middle of the belly of the Virgin with the left hand in which she carries the Ear of Grain, the tail of the Crow, parts of the tail of the Southern Water-Snake, and the right shoulder of the Ass; the shoulder of the Centaur and part of Pegasus. ¶ In the **second decan of Libra**. According to the Persians, a man being driven whose name is *Bredemis* of the fish, with whom is a wagon in which a man holds a whip in his hand and a red basket of another man with him, with colored *ostramantice exumene* <note>likely a corrupted Greek or Persian term for specific garments or tools</note>; yet for the people of the pharmacists and the perfumers; after this a man sitting in a litter, around him his companions; then a very small thing and the middle of the ship, the front part of the Centaur, the middle of the ship, and the middle of the Dragon with the middle of the Greater Bear together with a spring of water. According to the Indians, a man with the form of a vulture *Aukamie*, thirsty, with a wheel in his hand, wishing to fly through the air, desiring a wife and offspring. According to the Greeks, part of the tail of the Dragon; the end of the tail of the Greater Bear with the left leg of the Virgin's seat, and the horse of the Centaur; with the shoulder and chest. ¶ In the **third decan of Libra**. According to the Persians, the rear of the Dragon with the stern of the ship and the end of the Centaur and the Greater Bear; then the brain of the head; Hermes <note>Mercury</note> moved away from the head; then a naked man named *Arbedi* leaning on his elbow, having his left hand over his head, his right proceeding forward, then the horns of *Arbedi* above the heads of two men whose heads are burdened with twin horns, entwined with each other, whose name is *Estuarius*; after these things a certain thing named Heaven. According to the Indians, a man honors the face of a horse *Manticis*, carrying a bow and arrows in his hand, with one arrow already positioned, standing among the brush with the intention of hunting, a lone king considering the outcome. According to the Greeks, part of the tail of the Dragon, the hands of the Bear-Watcher <note>Arctophylax/Boötes</note> with the arm and right knee; and the end of the reins and the shackle of the Virgin, and also the left hand of the Centaur with the foot of the Wolf.

-><image-desc>A woodcut illustration of a scorpion viewed from above, with its tail curved to the side, representing the zodiac sign Scorpio.</image-desc><-

♏ **Scorpio**, phlegmatic by nature. It rises in its **first decan** as the Persians assert: the end of the male horse which is itself called *Bridenuss*, which they call the Centaur. With which is the end of the Bull; and at the same time a blackish thrower, in his hand a spear; and a thing called a cymbal. According to the Indians, a woman of fit stature, entirely suitable, carrying food because of hunger; fans...

and to walk through the lands. According to the Greeks, the arm of Arctophylax <note>The "Bear-Watcher," also known as Boötes</note> and part of the head of the Asses, the tail and head of Medusa <note>original: "caput gorgonis"</note> which they call Aloe, with the right arm; then the chest and shoulders of Libra, and the end of Alphecca <note>The brightest star in Corona Borealis</note>, that is, the crown of Ariadne; the end of the Wolf with its tail, and the tail of the Centaur.

### In the second decan of Scorpio ♏
According to the Persians, a naked man whose name is Assalius, and the middle of the Male <note>likely referring to a specific constellation figure</note> with the middle of Taurus. According to the Indians, an exiled girl with a calm face, naked and without clothing or money, her foot bound by a shackle; she tosses on the sea as if seeking the shores. According to the Greeks, the upper arm of the Lesser Bear with part of the Dragon's tail; also the spear of Medusa with the northern horn, the legs of Libra, and the foot with the claw of the Scorpion and the back of the Wolf.

### In the third decan of Scorpio ♏
According to the Persians, the front part of a huge male horse with its tail rolled back over its back, and the front part of Taurus with part of the Dog covered by a shell; they say he who pulls the reins holds twin snakes in his hand. According to the Indians, a furious wild dog, huge with whitening bristles, and various types of hunting sandals <note>possibly a corruption of a specific Indian term for hunting gear</note> living there, each fleeing from the other in mutual regard. According to the Greeks, the chest of the Lesser Bear and the coils of the Dragon; then the shoulder and right arm of the Kneeler <note>Hercules</note> with the seven right arms <note>unclear reading, possibly a reference to stars</note>, the belly of the Scorpion, the knot of the sides, and finally the head of the fire-bearing Censer <note>The constellation Ara</note>.

### Sagittarius ♐, double-bodied, divided in the middle.
In the first decan, as seen by the Persians: The form of an adorned naked man with a diminished head, shouting toward a ship, above whose head is a raven with its beak nearly touching the ship; then the body of a Dog covered by a shell, failing from head to tail. According to the Indians, a centaur, a naked man from the thigh upward; then a horse, in his hand a bow and arrows already placed upon the bow, with a loud shout aiming at a place with a branch for plundering with his own clothes.

<image-desc>A small woodcut illustration depicts a two-masted ship at sea on the left, with its sails furled. To the right of the ship stands a human figure in profile, possibly a sailor or mythological figure, wearing a garment that appears feathered or scaled, extending an arm toward the vessel.</image-desc>

According to the Greeks, the neck of the Lesser Bear with part of the Dragon, the haunch of Alcides <note>Hercules</note> with part of the back, also the head and part of the body of the kneeling Lamb, the right shoulder, the haunch with the left haunch and foot; then the sting of the Scorpion and the body of the Censer. 

### In the second decan of Sagittarius ♐
According to the Persians, Cerarafius <note>likely a corruption of Cepheus or a Persian name</note>, Perseus with his left hand striking the jaws of a cat, with his right pressing the horns of Capricorn, resisting a fierce dog with his right foot; on the head of the dog is the head of a hare and the head of a lion likewise, and half the body of a sailor and half a ship, with half a dolphin and the middle of the Coma Berenices <note>original: "astrocome"</note>. According to the Indians, a woman sitting on a camel, hairy and dressed in rags, with a *karcan*—that is, a felt garment—between her hands a small basket containing headbands. According to the Greeks, the Lesser Bear with part of the Dragon and the knee of Alcides; then two vultures; then the head of the Serpent-Bearer <note>Ophiuchus</note> with the shoulder and left hand, and part of the serpent, and the end of the Narcissus <note>possibly a star name</note> with the part where the hand holds the arrow and the iron of the arrow; and part of the Southern Beast.

### In the third decan of Sagittarius ♐
According to the Persians, a dog in whose mouth is the hand of Cassiopeia, and the end of the fierce dog with the hare and the rest of the lion's body and the remainder of the sailor; the second half of the ship and the dolphin with the tail of the Coma Berenices; then half of the Bear's wing and the wagon of the Greater Bear; then the Dragon and the serpent intertwined with each other. According to the Indians, a man of golden color wearing a cap and a tunic and clothed in the bark of a tree; in his hand are two wooden collars, and he sits upon a purple litter. According to the Greeks, the body of the Lesser Bear with part of the Dragon; also part of the Swan <note>original: "oloris"</note> which is also the Falling Vulture <note>Lyra</note>; and part of the tail of the serpent, the head of Sagittarius, the shoulder with the front foot, and part of the Southern Beast.

<meta>This page continues the astrological descriptions of the zodiac signs, specifically detailing the decans (ten-degree subdivisions) of Pisces and Aquarius according to the traditions of the Persians, Indians, and Greeks.</meta>

¶ ♓ **Pisces** <note>The sign of the Fishes</note> is of a round, imperfect form and of a dual nature. In its **first decan** <note>A ten-degree segment of a zodiac sign</note>, according to the opinion of the **Persians**, there rises the second half of the Great Bear <note>Ursa Major</note>, along with a water-woman whom they call *Bedas*, having the head of a huge fish. There also appears the beginning of a spring of harmful water, and the beginning of a beast of sorrow <note>original: "fere dolore"</note>, and the headless dog which the Persians call *Persesax*. According to the **Indians**, there is a black man with a hairy body like a wild boar, with teeth as long as a beam and sharp as thorns; he carries ox-leashes and is preparing to catch livestock and fish with nets. According to the **Greeks**, there is the half of the Lesser Bear <note>Ursa Minor</note>, part of the Dragon <note>Draco</note> with its neck, and the ends of *Azelfage* (that is, the tortoise) <note>original: "tarcuta"</note>, along with the end of the Hen <note>Cygnus</note> and part of its wings; also added is part of the chin of the Bear-Watcher <note>Arctophylax or Boötes</note>, the shepherd, and his horse.

<image-desc>Woodcut of a goat or ibex with long, curved horns, standing on a small patch of earth.</image-desc>

¶ In the **second decan** of ♓ **Pisces**: According to the **Persians**, there is a woman whom they call *Alzabre* and the Romans call *Julia*, sitting in a litter; with her is a vine tree, the middle of the huge fish, the middle of the harmful spring, and the middle of the deceitful beast; finally, half of the Wagon <note>The Big Dipper</note>. According to the **Indians**, there is a woman wrapped in black feathers and fine linen, possessing money refined by fire and worked in iron; with her are mussels and snails. According to the **Greeks**, there is the end of the Lesser Bear, part of the Dragon's tail joined to the middle, with the part of the body following the chest. Then the wing of the Hen with its neck and beak, then the Arrow <note>Sagitta</note>, then the body of the Eagle <note>Aquila</note>, and the horns of Capricorn which are without the Archer <note>Sagittarius</note>.

¶ In the **third decan** of ♓ **Pisces**: According to the **Persians**, there is the tail of the fish, followed by the end of the noxious spring and the end of the deceitful beast, and the second half of the Wagon; then half of a monstrous thing, *Subhani*, which they call *Mastar*, standing with a balance <note>scales</note> without a head, which it carries in its hand. According to the **Indians**, there is a woman of pleasing sight with black eyes, having slender hands, working at a complex task to make for herself ornate colors out of iron. According to the **Greeks**, there is the haunch of the Lesser Bear with the coil of the Dragon; then the right foot of the Hen with the left wing. Also the Dolphin <note>Delphinus</note> and the arms of the Water-Bearer <note>Aquarius</note>, and the middle of Capricorn with the tail of the fish.

¶ ♒ **Aquarius** <note>The Water-Bearer</note> is airy and watery. In its **first decan**, as the doctrine of the **Persians** relates, there is *Abudius* (in Arabic *Annamerum*, that is, the Panther) and the head of the Horse-Puller <note>Equuleus</note> whose name is the House of Delos; also the head of the Centaur <note>Centaurus</note> whom they call *Asmeat*, with his left hand raised above his head. Then there is a black-headed creature snatching fish from the waters. According to the **Indians**, there is a man in the form of an Ethiopian, of the essence of *Aukanne*, wrapped in a carpet; with him are bronze vessels for extracting gold, wine, and water. According to the **Greeks**, there is the tail of the Lesser Bear, along with the foot and right hand of Cepheus; the left foot of the Hen and the end of the left wing; the head of the Horse, the first head of the Water-Bearer with the right shoulder, the haunch of Capricorn, and the end of the Southern Fish <note>Piscis Austrinus</note>.

<image-desc>Woodcut of a man (Aquarius) wearing a tunic and boots, pouring liquid from two vessels into a basin on the ground.</image-desc>

¶ In the **second decan** of ♒ **Aquarius**: According to the **Persians**, there is the middle of the body of the Horse-Puller, grasping a bow with the left hand and a wild boar with the right, which he also kicks with his foot, in whose mouth are snakes; the middle of the Centaur with the wing of a diver-bird <note>original: "mergi"</note>, black-headed, and also a dragon. According to the **Indians**, there is a man with the face and form of an Ethiopian, on a horse, carrying a bow and a small spear in his hand, as well as boxes full of pearls and precious gems: hyacinth, emerald, and such...

...kind. According to the Greeks, the middle of the Lesser Bear <note>Ursa Minor</note> follows, along with the hip and haunch of Cepheus and his right shoulder; then the second bow and the handle of the vessel in the hand of Aquarius, with the haunch and middle body of the Southern Fish. ¶ In the third decan of Aquarius ♒: According to the Persians, there is a certain extraordinary bird which is the Hen <note>original: "gallina"; likely the constellation Cygnus</note>, and the hind part of the one pulling the horse, with the hind part of the Centaur and the end of the bird which they call *Alaien* <note>original: "alaien"; likely a Latinization of the Arabic 'al-’ayyuq' or similar star name</note>. According to the Indians, a fierce, deceitful black man with hairy ears, wearing a wreath of leaves, fruit, and resin on his head, laboring under a weight of money as if to move to another place. According to the Greeks, the hip and left shoulder of Cepheus, and the head with the hand of the "Architect of the Mind" <note>original: "archite mentis"; possibly a corruption of a mythological name</note>, part of the Urn with the right hand and left foot of Aquarius, and the head of the Southern Fish.

<image-desc>Two woodcut illustrations of fish, representing the zodiac sign Pisces. The upper fish faces right and the lower fish faces left, both depicted with detailed scales and fins.</image-desc>

¶ The sign of Pisces ♓ is a double and watery sign. In its first decan, as the authority of the Persians holds, is half of a winged horse—whose Roman name is Pegasus—and likewise the "Second Horse"; then the head of a stag-bull which the Persians call *Attramur*; in its hands are twin snakes. What appeared to Ptolemy <note>Claudius Ptolemy, the 2nd-century Greco-Egyptian astronomer</note> was the head of the Scorpion with twin snakes in its mouth. Then the beginning of the *Fondion*, in Arabic *Atahaz*: that is, the Crocodile, which some call the River and others the "Burnt Path" <note>original: "viam perustaz"; likely referring to the Via Combusta or a similar celestial region</note>. According to the Indians, a man elegantly dressed heading home, arranging a fire with a gripping iron hand, placing three fish before himself. According to the Greeks, the hind part of the Lesser Bear with the left arm of Cepheus; and the belly of the Second Horse with the beginning of the first Fish and part of the Urn. ¶ In the second decan of Pisces ♓: According to the Persians, the middle of the stag-bull in whose hands are snakes. For the Egyptian Fish, which Ptolemy hears as the middle of the Scorpion being carried by a snake; then there is seen the middle of the Crocodile or the River or the Burnt Path. According to the Indians, a woman beautiful of face and white of body, sailing the sea with her chest bound to the stern; with her are her kinsmen and acquaintances, she herself longing for the harbor. According to the Greeks, the leg of Cepheus with the foot; the foot of the Throne itself; the hand of Andromeda; the head of Cassiopeia; the hind part of the Second Horse with the hind part of the first Fish and the tail of the Whale <note>Cetus</note>. ¶ In the third decan of Pisces ♓: According to the Persians, the hind part of the stag-bull holding a lamb; just as Ficheus <note>likely a reference to a specific astrological author or source</note> estimates, it is the wild snake of the Scorpion. Then the end of the Crocodile; then the River or the Burnt Path; then the whole "Sordid One"; then the hind parts sitting before his eyes between his hands. According to the Indians, a man with outstretched feet with whom is a pregnant male <note>likely a mythological or symbolic figure</note> having an Ethiopian in his womb, standing on a rock, shouting for the gift of the first-born and fire. According to the Greeks, the back of the Throne, the breast of Andromeda, *Elecile Tereballe* <note>unclear technical terms, possibly corrupted star names</note>, part of the linen thread with the end of the Whale.

<image-desc>A large decorative drop cap 'H' featuring a stylized human face within the letter's form.</image-desc>

Here begin the inventions of various nations regarding the celestial forms rising throughout the whole circle above the face of the earth. Some of these are interpretations which the power of the innermost understanding of nature has produced throughout the whole world with long-standing observation. Much indeed has become known through certain prophetic-like means, whether to the divine celestial powers, or to those familiar with the human race, or those devoted to human necessity. The Persian and Indian peoples first produced these figures, which is easy to see daily for those who penetrate to the interior efficacy of the stars. For the consideration of the sphere which Ptolemy and the Greeks describe, as it first ordained, so now through the di...

...easily corrects the turned <note>original: "uersos," likely referring to corrupted or rotated manuscript copies</note> codices. For when they first observed these types of forms and their meanings in the world, the fountain of wisdom was diverted into two directions: from there, the Indians, seized by a celestial genius, climbed higher; from here, the Greeks, following the Egyptians, remained below in the starry region and adapted the distinct intervals of the stars to their own fables. Therefore, a knowledge of the former is necessary for measuring the movements of the stars, while a knowledge of the latter is necessary for receiving the powers of the stars.

Since the <term>decan</term> <note>A subdivision of a zodiac sign consisting of ten degrees</note> which arises through the signs has been explained, it is now following to measure the rising of the signs themselves through the various parts of the earth. For although all the signs are of equal dimension, the obliquity <note>tilt</note> of the circle connected to the earth does not allow for equal risings for everyone. <meta>This page continues a technical discussion of astronomical geography, specifically focusing on the rising times of zodiacal signs across different latitudes or 'climates'.</meta>

...namely, the inhabitants and those situated on the right sphere <note>original: "recti"; referring to the celestial sphere as seen from the equator</note> under the equinoctial line across the seven climates. There are two circles of the habitable zone which, by intersecting the zodiacal circle, measure the rising of the signs in equal quantity. One of these is the horizon, from which the signs emerge into the upper hemisphere and sink into the lower. The other is the meridian, which marks the poles of heaven and earth at an equal distance. Thus, the passage of the 12 signs in the right circle <note>the celestial equator</note> through each [meridian] maintains the same quantity of rising for each of the four [quadrants]. For through the two climates, they indeed rise in the same quantity. For the passage through the meridian is the same for everyone as that of the right circle. Therefore, there is a difference of half an hour between these risings of the signs through the individual climates; since various authors have existed regarding these, we have omitted the others because they are not correct.

---

We shall set out the risings of the signs according to the Alexandrians. In the right circle, therefore, Aries ♈ and Pisces ♓, as well as Libra ♎ and Virgo ♍, each rise in 27 degrees and 53 minutes <note>original: "punctis"; literally 'points', used here as minutes of arc</note>. Taurus ♉ and Aquarius ♒, and Scorpio ♏, rise in 29 degrees and 54 minutes. Gemini ♊ and Capricorn ♑ rise in 32 degrees and 13 minutes. Cancer ♋... the latitude of the lands is the distance they are from the equal line <note>the equator</note>; and this is also called the "altitude of the lands." For as much as the vernal circle <note>the celestial equator</note> is distant from the right circle, so much is the horizon depressed below the pole. We, however, take the latitude of the climates as the quantity of the whole space. Thus, the latitude of the **first climate** is from 1 degree up to 20 degrees and 27 minutes. In this part of the earth, the longest day is 13 equal hours. Therefore, in that part of the earth, Aries ♈ and Pisces ♓ each rise in 24 degrees and 20 minutes; Taurus ♉ and Aquarius ♒ in 27 degrees and 4 minutes; Gemini ♊ and Capricorn ♑ in 31 degrees and 6 minutes; Cancer ♋ and Sagittarius ♐ in 33 degrees and 17 minutes; Leo ♌ and Scorpio ♏ in 32 degrees and 20 minutes; Virgo ♍ and Libra ♎ in 31 degrees and 20 minutes. Saturn ♄ presides over this climate.

The latitude of the **second climate** is from 20 degrees and 27 minutes up to 27 degrees and 12 minutes. The rising of the signs in that climate is at a latitude of 23 degrees and 56 minutes. In this place, the longest day is 13 and a half hours. Therefore, in that part of the earth, Aries ♈ and Pisces ♓ rise in 22 degrees and 37 minutes; Taurus ♉ and Aquarius ♒ in 25 degrees and 38 minutes; Gemini ♊ and Capricorn ♑ in 30 degrees and 30 minutes; Cancer ♋ and Sagittarius ♐ in 34 degrees and 2 minutes; Leo ♌ and Scorpio ♏ in 34 degrees and 10 minutes; Virgo ♍ and Libra ♎ in 22 degrees and 3 minutes. For this climate, the Persians prefer Jupiter ♃; the Romans prefer the Sun ☉.

The latitude of the **third climate** (of Alexandria) is from 27 degrees and 12 minutes up to 33 degrees and 49 minutes. The rising of the signs is at a latitude of 30 degrees and 22 minutes. In this place, the longest day is 14 hours. There rise in that place Aries ♈ and Pisces ♓ in 20 degrees and 54 minutes; Taurus ♉ and Aquarius ♒ in 24 degrees and 12 minutes; Gemini ♊ and Capricorn ♑ in 29 degrees and 55 minutes; Cancer ♋ and Sagittarius ♐ in 34 degrees and 37 minutes; Leo ♌ and Scorpio ♏ in 35 degrees and 36 minutes; Virgo ♍ and Libra ♎ in 34 degrees and 47 minutes. To this climate, the Persians assigned Mars ♂, the Romans assigned Venus ♀.

The latitude of the **fourth climate** <note>original: "Quarti climatis"</note> extends from 33 degrees and 49 minutes <note>The text uses "puncta" (points) to refer to minutes of a degree.</note> up to 38 degrees and 46 minutes. The rising of the signs at the latitude of 36 degrees and 6 minutes, where the longest day is 14 and a half hours. There, Aries and Pisces rise at 19 degrees and 12 minutes. Taurus and Aquarius at 22 degrees and 46 minutes. Gemini and Capricorn at 29 degrees and 17 minutes. Cancer and Sagittarius at 35 degrees and 54 minutes. Leo and Scorpio at 37 degrees and 3 minutes. Virgo and Libra at 36 degrees and 27 minutes. According to the Persians, this climate belongs to the Sun; according to the Romans, it belongs to Jupiter.

The latitude of the **fifth climate** <note>The word "dracones" appears here in the source, likely a technical reference to the nodes of the moon or a specific astronomical table title.</note> extends from 38 degrees and 46 minutes up to 42 degrees and 58 minutes. The rising of the signs at the latitude of 40 degrees and 46 minutes, where the longest day is 15 hours. There, Aries and Pisces rise at 15 degrees and 32 minutes. Taurus and Aquarius at 21 degrees and 19 minutes. Gemini and Capricorn at 29 degrees and 37 minutes. Cancer and Sagittarius at 35 degrees and 53 minutes. Leo and Scorpio at 38 degrees and 6 minutes. Virgo and Libra at 37 degrees and 41 minutes. In the opinion of both [Persians and Romans], this climate belongs to Venus.

The latitude of the **sixth climate** extends from 42 degrees and 58 minutes up to 47 degrees and two minutes. The rising of the signs at the latitude of 45 degrees and 21 minutes, where the longest day is 15 and a half hours. There, Aries and Pisces rise at 15 degrees and 55 minutes. Taurus and Aquarius at 19 degrees and 53 minutes. Gemini and Capricorn at 27 degrees and 56 minutes. Cancer and Sagittarius at 36 degrees and 37 minutes. Leo and Scorpio at 39 degrees and 54 minutes. Virgo and Libra at 39 degrees and 45 minutes. The Persians consider Mercury to be the lord of this climate, but the Romans judge it to be the Moon.

The latitude of the **seventh climate** extends from 47 degrees and two minutes up to 63 degrees. Moreover, the rising of the signs in this same climate at the latitude of 48 degrees and 32 minutes, where the longest day for that part of the earth is 16 hours. Therefore, for that part of the earth, Aries and Pisces rise at 14 degrees and 33 minutes. Taurus and Aquarius at 18 degrees and 41 minutes. Gemini and Capricorn at 27 degrees and 18 minutes. Cancer and Sagittarius at 37 degrees and 15 minutes. Leo and Scorpio at 41 degrees and 6 minutes. Virgo and Libra at 41 degrees and 6 minutes. The Persians record the Moon as the lord of this climate; the Romans record Mars.

--- Page 88 ---
<meta>This page begins a technical discussion on the geometric and harmonic relationships between zodiac signs, following the general introduction to the celestial sphere.</meta>

**H**aving established these principles, it is now appropriate to measure the aspects <note>original: "respectus"; the way planets or signs "look at" one another</note> throughout the entire circle of the signs. Indeed, the aspects of the signs correspond to one another either by geometric division or by the numerical proportion of their degrees. Chords <note>straight lines connecting two points on a curve</note> divide the circle through geometric section: these are either equal or proportional, such as the multiple and super-particular proportions of numbers <note>mathematical ratios where one number contains another plus a fraction</note> which are consistent with harmonic bonds. Therefore, by this kind of harmonious proportionality, astrologers measure the aspects of the degrees.

This is done by a threefold rule. First, so that the number of degrees indicates the total sum of the circle. Second, so that the number of degrees completes the number of signs measuring the whole circle. Third, so that the number of degrees measures the chord of the arc measuring the whole circle, upon which any figure contained within the circle—having equal sides and angles—becomes proportional to the circle itself. There are, therefore, seven such aspects of the signs: the opposition, two trines <note>trigonus</note>, two quartiles <note>tetragonus; also known as squares</note>, and two sextiles <note>exagoni</note>. The opposition is formed across the diameter of the circle. The trine [occupies] a third part of the circle...

<summary>This page explains the mathematical and geometric foundations of astrological aspects, detailing how the circle is divided into harmonic proportions like the trine, square, and sextile. It emphasizes the relationship between geometry, musical harmony, and celestial measurement.</summary>

<keywords>astrology, geometry, harmonics, zodiac aspects, opposition, trine, quartile, sextile, celestial measurement</keywords>

--- Page 89 ---
<meta>The previous page likely introduced the division of the celestial circle; this page continues the mathematical and musical justification for the astrological aspects (opposition, trine, square, and sextile).</meta>

cuts. The **Square** <note>original: *Tetragonus*</note> represents a quarter-circle; the **Sextile** <note>original: *exagonus*</note> a sixth part. In all of these, that threefold moderation is found. For the degree of **Opposition** (180 degrees) counts the circle twice. Likewise, they complete the number of signs which, when taken twice, makes 12. This same line cuts the circle through the middle so that on both sides above the diameter, the semicircles contain figures of equal sides and angles.

The arc of the **Trine** <note>original: *Trigonalis*</note> chord is 120 degrees, which is a third of the circle. Likewise, it consists of four signs which, when taken three times, complete the 12; above the chord, an equilateral and equiangular triangle is contained within the circle. The portion of the **Square** side is 90 degrees, which is a quarter of the circle. The same consists of three signs counted four times: which, drawn into itself, renders a square contained by the circle. The arc of the **Sextile** chord is 60 degrees, which measures the circle when taken six times. Likewise, two signs taken six times make 12 above the chord. The hexagon contained in the circle is equiangular and equilateral.

Therefore, every circle is itself a multiple; for the arcs and chords themselves observe a proportionality among one another, partly of multiples and partly of <term>superparticulars</term> <note>A ratio where the larger number contains the smaller plus one unit of the smaller, such as 3:2 or 4:3</note>, such as 180 being equal to itself, 120 being double, and 90 being triple. And in this manner, regarding their angles at the center or at the circumference: the trine is double, the sextile is acute, and the obtuse angles of their bases—which are chords of the same circle—preserve this proportionality.

**Musicians** indeed call the half and the third the "greater numbers," because they render the <term>double</term> <note>2:1 ratio</note> and <term>sesquialter</term> <note>3:2 ratio</note> proportions, which are the most moderate harmonies: the **Octave** <note>original: *dyapason*</note> and the **Fifth** <note>original: *dyapente*</note>. For this reason, the circle first had to be cut through the middle because nothing fits one half that does not fit the other; the half of which produces the square. A third produces the hexagon, which when doubled completes the trine—namely a third of the circle—in proportion to the half, besides these.

Others borrow the reasons for these types of sectional arcs from the very behavior of the stars, asserting that it is cut by the diameter because the light of the stars is in opposition to the Sun; so much so that the Moon increases in light up to the opposition, at which point it is full, and then decreases. They have commented on the **Square** for this reason: that any star, whenever it is ninety degrees distant from its seat, varies in its motion. The **Trine**, however, is derived from the two superior stars <note>Mars, Jupiter, or Saturn</note> which, whenever they are about 120 degrees distant from the Sun, change their motion between direct and retrograde. The **Sextile** side was half the diameter, as far as the houses of Venus are distant from the houses of the Luminaries <note>the Sun and Moon</note>.

For these reasons, therefore, we divide the circle into these sections: so that whatever degree is rising, whatever other degree is on any of its lines is thus proportional in respect to it. For example, when the first degree of **Aries** is rising, the first of **Gemini** is in its sextile; the first of **Cancer** is in its square; the first of **Leo** is in its trine; and the first of **Libra** is opposite; and so on the other side in opposition to these. Thus, these signs are moved in relation to each other. For the others are called "adverse." Since, therefore, there is contrariety in the **Opposition**, and the **Square** is a half-opposition, they have reckoned these two as the "inimical" <note>unfriendly or harsh</note> part. But because the **Trine** always falls into a sign of the same nature, and the **Sextile** is half of a trine, these two are partly considered friendly...

<summary>This page explains the geometric, musical, and astronomical justifications for astrological aspects, linking the angles of the trine, square, and sextile to harmonic intervals and planetary motions.</summary>
<keywords>geometry, astrology, musical harmony, octave, fifth, zodiac signs, planetary aspects, opposition, trine, square, sextile</keywords>

--- Page 90 ---
<meta>The catchword from the previous page indicates a transition into the discussion of signs that correspond through opposition, hexagons, and squares.</meta>

...yielded. To these points, certain objectors raise a challenge: If the circle is divided into those numbers by which they themselves are counted, why then do the five-twelfths <note>original: "quincunx"</note>, the eight-twelfths, the ten-twelfths, and so on, not also divide the same circle? To these we respond easily: because to no others, except those we have mentioned, does the triple moderation apply. Therefore, the others being rejected, these alone have prevailed.

### ¶ Chapter four: On friendly signs.

<image-desc>A large ornamental drop cap 'A' featuring woodcut designs of fruit and foliage. The letter 'A' is white against a dark, detailed background.</image-desc>

**By** this method of dividing the circle, we shall weigh certain relationships of the signs among themselves: for some are friendly, while others are indeed inimical. Furthermore, some rise directly, others indirectly. This classification usually results in something significant. Most of those that look upon each other in a triangle <note>original: "trigono"; a trine aspect of 120 degrees</note> or a hexagon <note>original: "exagono"; a sextile aspect of 60 degrees</note> are friendly. The distinction between them is that the hexagon favors with a certain affection, while the triangle supports with the greatest zeal. Those that look upon each other in opposition or in a square <note>original: "tetragono"; a quadrature aspect of 90 degrees</note> are inimical. And the difference between these is that the square detracts as if with a certain envy, while the opposition harms with the heaviest hatred.
¶ Those are said to rise "directly" which rise extended in a straight line; they are from the beginning of Cancer to the end of Sagittarius. Those are said to rise "obliquely" which rise twisted and curved; they are from the curve of Capricorn to the end of Gemini. Whence the former rise in more than 30 degrees and more than two hours, while the latter rise in less. It happens to these that those which rise obliquely "obey," in a proportional way, those rising directly, provided they enjoy a friendly aspect: such as Gemini to Libra, Taurus to Leo, and Cancer to Virgo, and so on in this manner.

### On signs congruent by nature, distance, power, and path.

<image-desc>A large ornamental drop cap 'D' containing a central woodcut of a five-petaled flower (rosette) with surrounding decorative flourishes.</image-desc>

**Next** there are also other relationships of the signs, distinguished into three types. ¶ The first is the equality of the signs' distance from the midheaven circle, which they call the circle of signs <note>the Zodiac</note>. Likewise, there is an equality of rising, such that the end of one and the beginning of the other, and the middle to the middle, rise equally; such is the relationship of Aries with Pisces, Taurus with Aquarius, and so on in order from the equinoctial points. ¶ The second part is the power of pairs, which is called <term>almukaurat</term> <note>A Latinization of an Arabic astrological term referring to signs of equal power or "commanding/obeying" signs</note>. These are those between which the end of one and the beginning of the other, and middle to middle, are equal. Such is the relationship of Gemini with Cancer, Taurus with Leo, and so on in order from the solstitial points. The third is the fellowship of signs with the same ruler, such as Aries and Scorpio <note>both ruled by Mars</note>, Taurus and Libra <note>both ruled by Venus</note>, Cancer and Leo <note>ruled by the Moon and Sun</note>, and so on in this manner.

¶ Regarding signs corresponding in opposition and hexagon to the square.

<summary>This page explains the geometric relationships (aspects) between zodiac signs, classifying them as friendly or inimical based on their degree of separation. it also introduces the concepts of direct and oblique rising and the "commanding" relationships between signs of equal power.</summary>
<keywords>astrology, zodiac aspects, trine, sextile, square, opposition, direct rising, oblique rising, almukaurat, planetary rulers</keywords>

--- Page 91 ---
From this it happens: that although sometimes by a certain occasion of their rising they are placed in opposition, some fall into a sextile <note>original: "exagonum"; an astrological aspect where planets are 60 degrees apart, generally considered harmonious</note> which by no means regard each other, yet this opposition does not harm, and this sextile is beneficial. Concerning this kind of opposition, from the first kinship are Gemini, Capricorn, Cancer, and Sagittarius. From the second: Aries and Virgo, Libra and Pisces. From the third: Aries and Scorpio, Taurus and Libra. Concerning this kind of sextile, from the first kinship are Aries and Pisces, Virgo and Libra. From the second: Gemini and Cancer, Capricorn and Sagittarius. From the third: Capricorn, Aries, Cancer, Capricorn, and Sagittarius <note>The text repeats the symbols for Aries, Cancer, Capricorn, and Sagittarius</note>. To this it must be added that a square <note>original: "tetragonus"; an aspect of 90 degrees, usually considered challenging</note> which falls into this kind of kinship is found to be less harmful. In which square, from the first kinship are Taurus and Aquarius, Leo and Scorpio. From the second: Aries, Cancer, Capricorn, and Sagittarius. From the third: Gemini and Virgo, Sagittarius and Pisces.

### Concerning the years, months, days, and hours of the signs.

Next, we shall arrange the years, months, days, and hours of the signs. Their number is found in two ways. First, indeed, so that through the individual rising of the climates <note>referring to the geographical latitudes or zones of the earth used in ancient astronomy</note> individual years are compared, through five points individual months, and in this manner days and hours. Second, so that the minor years and months of its lord <note>the ruling planet of the zodiac sign</note> are counted for each of the signs. However, days and hours are found in two ways. First, indeed, so that the minor years of its lord are multiplied by 12, and these are the months; when these are doubled, the number of those same years is added. Therefore, the whole sum being divided by 10, however many are collected are the days; if anything less than 10 remains, it is a part of a day. These, therefore, are the days and hours of that sign. In the second way, so that the minor years of its star <note>planet</note> are resolved into months, first a half is taken away. Then from the remainder, the number of those same years is subtracted. The residue, therefore, divided by 24, returns the days. If indeed less remains, they are hours. In these ways, therefore, the years, months, days, and hours of the signs having been found are computed through the individual signs.

# On the guidance of the signs over diverse lands.

<image-desc>A decorative square woodcut initial 'O' opens the main body of text, containing floral motifs and stylized leaves.</image-desc>

Our geographers divide the world into two parts: they trace Asia from the rising of the sun <note>the East</note> between both oceans toward the west as far as the Mediterranean Sea. Here, Europe and Africa begin. They bound Europe between the Mediterranean Sea and the Northern Ocean, ending in the west at the islands of Gades <note>modern-day Cádiz, Spain</note>; Africa they enclose in the south and west by Mount Atlas. ¶ Other parts are numbered as 15 in a general division: India, Parthia, Mesopotamia, Syria; after these the Greater Pentapolis, Egypt, the regions beyond, Bactria, Lesser Scythia, Hyrcania, Albania, Armenia, both parts of Cappadocia, Hibernia <note>likely a transcription error for Iberia, given the context of Asia Minor</note>, and finally Asia Minor. Of these, the specific divisions are: of India, the eastern part is Ethiopia <note>in antiquity, "Ethiopia" often referred generally to distant southern/eastern lands</note>; of Parthia there are 5: Tigris, Aratubia, Assyria, Media, and Persia; of Mesopotamia there are 3: Babylonia, Chaldea, and Arabia. Of the latter, there are two parts: Nabataea and Saba. Of Syria there are three: Commagene and Phoenicia—which has two parts, Tyre and Sidon—and then Palestine. The latter has 4 parts: Judea, Samaria, Galilee, and Philistia. ¶ Of Asia Minor there are 10: Bithynia (or Mygdonia), Galatia, Phrygia, Lycaonia, Caria, Lydia (or Meonia), Pamphylia, Isauria, Cilicia, and Lycia. ¶ The parts of Europe in the general division are 7: Greater Scythia, Germany with Moesia, Thrace, Greece, Pannonia, Istria, Italy, Gaul, and Spain. The subdivisions of these are: of Scythia, 2: Alania and Dacia (or Gothia); of Germany, two: Alemania and Teutonia; of Thrace, 2: Noricum and Raetia; of Greece, 4: Dalmatia, Epirus, Illyria (or Dardania), and Attica—which has two parts, Boeotia and the Peloponnese—then Thessaly, which has two parts, Pieria and Arcadia; then Macedonia, Achaia, and Lacedaemonia. Of Istria there are three parts: the Maritime, the Mountainous, and in the middle **our homeland, Carinthia** <note>The author identifies himself as being from Carinthia, a region in modern-day Austria/Slovenia</note>. Of Italy, 4: Tuscany, Etruria, Apulia, and Campania. Of Gaul, 3: Belgica, Raetia, and Aquitaine. ¶ The parts of Africa are 7: Lesser Libya, Pyracuum (or Zengis), Carthage, Numidia, Gaetulia, Mauritania, and Western Ethiopia. Of Lesser Libya there are three parts: Cyrenaica, Pentapolis, and Tripolis. Of Mauritania there are two: Sitifensis and Tingitana. Between all these, various other nations remain scattered throughout the world, such as the Ichthyophagi <note>literally "fish-eaters"</note>, the Pangisi, the Barbarians, the Troglodytes <note>cave-dwellers</note>, the Slavs, the Vandals, the Scots, the Britons, and others of that kind divided by mid-way locations such as islands, mountains, marshes, and sands. Of all these, there are innumerable other subdivisions determined by their own nations and distinguished by the names in their own languages. For all these have different names across different tongues. Therefore, it is impossible to fit these to Latin names from Arabic names unless they are first known by their boundaries. This is especially true since they are demonstrated here not in any strict order, but in scattered commentaries. For neither Albumasar <note>Abu Ma'shar, a famous 9th-century Persian astrologer</note> nor any other distributed them among the dominions of the signs except in a scattered way and by name only. For this reason, we have set this down beforehand, so that the places and sites may be known from their names; and if we are less able to adapt them, any studious person embracing these things may have a path for learning them thoroughly.

<meta>This page continues the discussion of astrological influences on the human form and health, specifically detailing the "melothesia" (the mapping of zodiac signs to body parts) and the signs governing physical beauty and character.</meta>

After this, we consider those signs which lead to the human likeness <note>original: "hominum speciem"; referring to signs depicted as human figures</note>. These are Aries, Gemini, Leo, and the first half of Sagittarius. Their order is such that Gemini and Leo are among the highest, and with the middle of Sagittarius, they are among the average: as is commonly said. However, the human race is also distinguished among the signs in another way: for the fiery triplicity <note>A group of three zodiac signs belonging to the same element</note> presides over the first rank, the airy over the second, and so on in order.

### Concerning the domains of the signs in the partitioning of the body.

Now the human body itself <gloss>must be distinguished among the domains of the signs regarding the body</gloss> of man. 

¶ **Aries** rules the head along with the face and the sharpness of the eyes, as well as their ailments, along with conditions affecting the ears and eyes. 
¶ **Taurus** rules the neck and throat with their ailments: to which belong the glands and fistulas <note>abnormal passages in the flesh</note>; a hump on the back, eye pain, and polyps, along with foul breath. 
¶ **Gemini** are the shoulder blades and shoulders along with the arms, forearms, and hands, and their injuries. 
¶ **Cancer** rules the chest, lungs, esophagus, and spleen, along with the "necks" of the eyes <note>likely the optic nerves or muscles</note>, spots on the eyes, and ophthalmia <note>inflammation of the eye</note>; and whatever harms the chest internally. 
¶ **Leo** rules the upper opening of the stomach—which our people call the "heart" <note>the cardia</note>—and the pair of nerves, the muscles, the bones of the back, and their ailments. 
¶ **Virgo** rules the belly with the intestines and the spleen down to the anus, and their discomforts. 
¶ **Libra** rules the thighs and from the navel down to the buttock, along with the loins, the kidneys, and the small vessels of the kidneys, and their ailments. 
¶ **Scorpio** rules the buttock, the groin, the marrow, the spine with the anus and the bladder together, and their ailments such as difficulty in excretion: such as ileus <note>intestinal obstruction</note>, tenesmus <note>straining</note>, strangury <note>painful urination</note>, as well as hemorrhoids, stones, abscesses, cancer, and "argenia" <note>likely a corruption of 'argema', a white ulcer on the eye, though here listed with lower-body ailments</note>. 
¶ **Sagittarius** rules the hips; then spots, superfluity of limbs <note>extra digits or growths</note>, then also injury to the bones: fractures, falling headlong, and the bites or any injury from wild beasts and serpents. 
¶ **Capricorn** rules the knees with their sinews, as well as bleary-eyedness. 
¶ **Aquarius** rules the legs down to the ankles; then obstructions of the veins, jaundice, and the discomforts of melancholy. 
¶ **Pisces** rules the feet with their sinews, as well as obstructions of the sinews, gout, and swelling.

### Concerning the signs leading to dignity of form, to generosity, and to the fulfillment of union.

Next, we must distinguish those things which designate certain advantages and disadvantages, both of the mind and the body, as well as external accidents. For there are signs that lead to dignity and beauty of form. This occurs whenever they rise at the moment of birth, or host the Lord of the Ascendant, or the Moon, or the **Almuten** <term>The planet with the most dignity or strength in a specific degree of the zodiac</term> of the birth, who is the principal leader. These signs are Aries, Gemini, Leo, Virgo, Libra, Sagittarius, and Aquarius; these same signs also lead to benevolence, gentleness, and greatness of soul. Indeed, those which provide and fulfill are the Aries triplicity. Therefore, the "consuming" signs, whenever they are placed adversely in the circle at birth and are hindered by misfortunes, produce those who are wasteful and extravagant...

they bring forth: and perhaps they prohibit all fortune and gain. If these signs stand in a friendly relationship to the fortunate planets, they hinder the pursuit of life's needs and restrain immoderate expenses. Truly, those signs which provide abundance—being blessed by both the convenience of their location and the fortunate planets—bring a wealth of resources; however, when corrupted by misfortunes in an adverse location, they portend causes of harm to one's wealth. ¶ Others distinguish the form and habit of women: for Taurus ♉, Leo ♌, Scorpio ♏, and Aquarius ♒ lead to a dignified form and an honest habit. Conversely, Virgo ♍, Capricorn ♑, Gemini ♊, Cancer ♋, Libra ♎, and Scorpio ♏, being bipartite <note>Divided into two parts or natures.</note>, occupy a middle ground.

¶ Others are of many offspring, such as Cancer ♋, Pisces ♓, and half of Sagittarius ♐ and Aquarius ♒. Truly, Aries ♈, Taurus ♉, Libra ♎, Sagittarius ♐, and Pisces ♓ have few offspring. Entirely sterile are Gemini ♊, Leo ♌, and Virgo ♍, and especially Taurus ♉.

¶ Others relate to severed limbs and much sharpness of mind. Regarding severed limbs: Aries ♈, Taurus ♉, Leo ♌, and Pisces ♓. Regarding much anger and sharpness: Aries ♈, Leo ♌, and Scorpio ♏.

Capricorn ♑ and Aquarius ♒ are low. The <term>trine</term><note>A "trigonus" or trine is an astrological aspect of 120 degrees.</note> of Cancer ♋ is without voice. For in these, if Saturn ♄ should be corrupted, it weakens the voice and hearing of the newborn and leaves them mute and deaf.

¶ Others lead to inconveniences of the body, such as <term>abscesses</term><note>original: "apostemata"</note>, spots, the itch, and filth, while also denying voice and hearing. There are five in total: Aries ♈, Cancer ♋, Scorpio ♏, Capricorn ♑, and Pisces ♓, whenever they carry either the Moon ☽ at birth, or the Part of Fortune, or the <term>Part of Algaib</term><note>original: "partem algaib", an Arabic astrological term referring to the "Part of the Hidden" or "Part of the Unseen".</note>, that is, the intrinsic part of all things.

¶ There are certain locations determined through the signs which prepare defects of the eyes. First among the Pleiades in Taurus ♉; second, the place of the "little cloud" in Cancer ♋; two among the stars of Scorpio ♏; fifth, the place of the Arrow in Sagittarius ♐; sixth, at the spine of Capricorn ♑; seventh, at the handle in Sagittarius; besides these, sometimes Leo ♌ with Libra ♎ is harmful to the sight.

¶ The location in Taurus ♉ at 13 degrees and 36 minutes, up to 14 degrees and 30 minutes, at a northern latitude from 3 degrees up to 15. ¶ The location in Cancer ♋ at the 21st degree and 8th minute, at a northern latitude of 42 minutes. ¶ In Scorpio ♏, one in the 20th degree, the other in the 21st degree and 10th minute, at a northern latitude of 6 degrees. ¶ The location in Sagittarius ♐ at the 15th degree and 20th minute, at a southern latitude of 6 degrees and 20 minutes. ¶ The location in Capricorn ♑ at the 22nd degree, at a northern latitude of 39 degrees and 15 minutes. Around the handle in Sagittarius ♐, four stars from 20 degrees and 10 minutes up to 24 degrees and 20 minutes.

It must be known, therefore, that these degrees and minutes of these locations existed in the days of <term>Albumasar</term><note>Abu Ma'shar (787–886 AD), a leading Persian astrologer of the Abbasid court in Baghdad.</note> as he himself asserts: that is, in the year 1100 of the Alexandrian era. In this our time, however, that is, in the year of the incarnation of the Lord 1400 <note>The text likely refers to the year 1400 or 1403, adjusting for the precession of the equinoxes.</note>, they are three degrees less six minutes by the law of their journey through the <term>equinoxes</term><note>original: "omotos", likely a corruption of "omotos" or "homotos" referring to the motion of the spheres.</note>; besides these, there are others through the signs and degrees corruptive to the eyes, which we shall set forth in their proper places.

¶ Others draw toward vices of the soul, such as deceit, anxiety, wickedness, atrocity, and violence; toward ferocity, deceit, wickedness, and hypocrisy are Leo ♌, Sagittarius ♐, Capricorn ♑, and Pisces ♓; toward anxiety are Scorpio ♏ and Capricorn ♑; Virgo ♍ and Libra ♎ are moderate within.

¶ Others distinguish the types of birds, quadrupeds, and reptiles. For Virgo ♍, Libra ♎, Scorpio ♏, Sagittarius ♐, and Pisces ♓ are birds of the second and third <term>decan</term><note>A decan is a subdivision of a zodiac sign, covering 10 degrees.</note> of Capricorn ♑. <meta>This page continues the technical classification of zodiac signs, moving from their physical associations (animals and plants) to their elemental qualities, geographical directions, and the structure of the astrological houses.</meta>

Aries ♈ and Leo ♌, along with the second half of Sagittarius ♐ and the first half of Capricorn ♑, are four-footed animals <note>Latin: *quadrupedia*</note>. Among these, there is a distinction: Aries ♈, Taurus ♉, and Leo ♌ are four-footed with hooves; Capricorn ♑ has claws; and the second half of Sagittarius ♐ has heels. Next, Cancer ♋, Scorpio ♏, and Capricorn ♑ are creeping things <note>Latin: *reptilia*</note>, and among these, Scorpio ♏ includes worms. For the watery signs preside over aquatic creatures, of which Cancer ♋, Pisces ♓, and Scorpio ♏ govern snails and creeping things. ¶ Other signs govern types of trees and sprouts. For Gemini ♊, Leo ♌, and Aquarius ♒ govern tall trees; Virgo ♍, Scorpio ♏, and the first part of Pisces ♓ govern low-growing plants and shrubs. The Earth Trigon <note>The group of three earth signs: Taurus, Virgo, and Capricorn</note> nourishes seeds. Specifically, Taurus ♉ governs graftings, Virgo ♍ governs sown crops, and Capricorn ♑ governs potherbs. ¶ Others govern waters and fiery things. Cancer ♋ indeed governs rainwaters; Scorpio ♏ and Pisces ♓ govern flowing streams, while Pisces ♓ also governs stagnant pools. All fiery things belong to Aries ♈, Leo ♌, and Sagittarius ♐. ¶ It is fitting to distinguish the regions of the leading signs. The Fiery Trigon belongs to the East: whence Aries ♈ is the heart of the East, Leo ♌ is to the left, and Sagittarius ♐ is to the right. For this reason, Aries ♈ is the East Wind <note>Latin: *subsolanus*</note>, Leo ♌ is the South-East Wind <note>Latin: *eurus*</note>, and Sagittarius ♐ is the South-East-by-East Wind <note>Latin: *vulturnus*</note>. The Earth Trigon belongs to the South: whence Capricorn ♑ is the heart of the South, Taurus ♉ is to the left, and Virgo ♍ is to the right. For this reason, Capricorn ♑ is the South Wind <note>Latin: *auster*</note>, Taurus ♉ is the Southern Wind <note>Latin: *notus*</note>, and Virgo ♍ is the South-West Wind <note>Latin: *affricus australis*</note>. The Airy Trigon is Western: whence Libra ♎ is the heart of the West, Gemini ♊ is to the right, and Aquarius ♒ is to the left. Therefore, Libra ♎ is the West Wind <note>Latin: *fauonius*</note> and Aquarius ♒ is the North-West Wind <note>Latin: *zephirus*</note>, while Gemini ♊ is the North-West-by-West Wind <note>Latin: *chorus*</note>. The Watery Trigon is Northern: whence Cancer ♋ is the heart of the North, Pisces ♓ is to the right, and Scorpio ♏ is to the left; therefore Cancer ♋ is the North Wind <note>Latin: *boreas*</note>, Scorpio ♏ is the North-North-West Wind <note>Latin: *circinus*</note>, and Pisces ♓ is the North-East Wind <note>Latin: *aquilo*</note>.

->¶ Concerning the signs leading to diseases and their occasions.<-

<image-desc>Decorative square woodcut initial 'Q' with a central floral motif.</image-desc>Since we have set forth the natural leadership of the signs as was necessary, it follows that we should next investigate their accidental leadership. Among these, the first thing that occurs is the partition of the four circles, then the series of the twelve houses <note>Latin: *domiciliorum*</note>. For since the highest circle perpetually encompasses the world from the east through the west every single day, it brings back all the remaining things contained below in a complete circuit. Whence it is necessary that in every hour, individual signs rise through different parts of the earth, and at the same time set for others, fixing the pivot <note>Latin: *cardinem*</note> of the sky for some and the earth for others. Between these, the quadrants of the circle are designated, each subdivided by three intervals called "houses," just as the year was fourfold and the signs themselves were twelve. There are, therefore, two quadrants from the pivot of the earth up to the tenth house, which are masculine, approaching, and eastern; the remaining two are feminine, receding, left-handed, and western. To some, it has seemed that the hemisphere above the earth is the right side and the subterranean is the left; and thus they arrange these same quadrants through the four parts of the world. Of these quadrants, the first parts are called "pivots" <note>Latin: *cardines*; the angular houses</note>, the second are "succeding," and the third are "remote" <note>cadent</note>; because the first confirm a matter, the second permit it, and the third deny it. Their numerical names are taken in order: the first is the East <note>the Ascendant</note>, which is followed by the second, then the third, and so on in order up to the twelfth. Each is named in the second place according to its own specific effects.

<keywords>zodiac signs, trigones, classical winds, astrological houses, cardines, four elements, botanical astrology, animal classification</keywords>

<meta>This page begins a detailed explanation of the twelve astrological houses, following the visual diagram provided in the source. It establishes the specific life areas governed by each segment of the sky.</meta>

->**Figure of the twelve houses of heaven**<-
<note>original: "Figura duodecim domorum celi". This refers to the square diagram described in the image metadata, which serves as a map of the sky at a specific moment.</note>

The East <note>The First House, or Ascendant</note> therefore presides over life, the body, the soul, and the origin and motion of all things. The **Second** is the house of substance, leading toward acquisitions, profits, possessions, and the duties of giving and receiving loans. 

¶ The **Third** concerns brothers and sisters; it regards neighbors and kinsmen, as well as counsel, laws, judgment, controversies, and disputations. It also partakes in travel, mandates, messengers, dreams, and movements. 

¶ The **Fourth** are the fathers, parents, lineage, roots, and "wateriness" <note>likely referring to irrigation or bodies of water on land</note>; the seizing of fields, cities, fortresses, and buildings. Also hidden and concealed things, subterranean places, treasures, the end of matters—that is, death and the remains of the dead—as well as serpents; and those things which are said to be brought in <note>original: "ingestione"</note>. It is a participant in a threefold administration: such as digging, burning, and stripping, and others of that kind. 

¶ The **Fifth** is the house of offspring, which furthermore governs clients, gifts, pleasures, and delights, and partakes in the fruits of honor, friendship, and hope. 

¶ Of the **Sixth** are illnesses, their causes, states, and conditions; male and female servants, wickedness, injustice, and it partakes in local movement itself. 

¶ The **Seventh** are women, betrothals, groomsmen, and the participation in controversies <column-break/> <note>The text cuts off mid-word "oppo-", likely "opposition" or "opponents", continuing on the next page.</note>

<meta>This page continues the systematic explanation of the twelve astrological houses (domicilia) and their planetary rulers, likely following a discussion of the first six houses or the general theory of the celestial sphere.</meta>

...suspicions, respects, and everything that is sought, such as thieves, fugitives, and lost items; the place and person by whom a journey is prepared. ¶ The **eighth** designates death, deadly poisons, fear, and everything lost and irrecoverable; also inheritances, leisure, laziness, sloth, fraud, inactivity, madness, desperation, and wrath. ¶ The **ninth** are long journeys, exiles, then honesty, justice, truth, temperance, prudence, observance, religion, laws, temples, ceremonies, philosophy, theology, and the other sciences, divinations, scriptures, envy <note>original: "inuicia", likely a scribal error for "invidia" (envy) or "indicia" (signs)</note>, visions, and necessary matters. ¶ The **tenth** are dignities, kingdoms, princedoms, dominions, powers, judges, eloquence, voices, skills, works, mothers, and assemblies with kings. ¶ The **eleventh** are fortunes, hope, friendship, grace, resources and supports, the patronage of kings, and returns. ¶ The **twelfth** are enemies, labor, sorrow, anguish, punishments, envy, detraction, fraud, deceit, hypocrisy, prisons, captives, falls, degradation, ignominy, ruin; finally, beasts of burden and wild animals. Therefore, the writers of this art seem to have rightly borrowed these names and these properties of the houses from the order, nature, and properties of the stars and circles.

¶ Rightly indeed is the origin and life of things commended to the **East** <note>The Ascendant or First House</note>; because it comes forth from darkness into light. ¶ First of all, it likens this to the fact that we are similarly beginning to come into the light of all things; as the first conceptions of our life are heard from above, it begins the span of the whole life you expect. For just as those things which are born burst forth from hiding, so the East emerges from the lower to the upper hemisphere. ¶ **Mercury** <note>represented by the symbol ☿</note> is second in order; since there are twin fortunes, as they are the wealth and sustenance of life—the necessity of which follows the birth in the first place—the house of substance was established second after the East. ¶ **Mars** <note>represented by ♂</note> however is third, just as **Saturn** <note>represented by ♄</note> had obtained the leadership of the kinship of brothers and relatives according to the order; and the third house stood out for them as it had been assigned. ¶ **The Sun** <note>represented by ☉</note> truly, with its monthly union with **the Moon** <note>represented by ☽</note>, acting in the manner of a male upon a female, exists as the cause of the act of generations and performs the role of the father. Since, therefore, the law of order required those same leaderships from the fourth from the East, the house of parents was located there. ¶ **Venus** <note>represented by ♀</note> moreover, in the part of fortune next after **Jupiter** <note>represented by ♃</note>; and since after the necessities of life (which are Jupiter) the next fortune in the world of man is pleasure, sexual union, offspring, and joys of this kind, she obtained the leadership of these things. Therefore, the order required that the fourth <note>likely meaning the fifth house in the sequence being described</note> be of her leadership; from which, because offspring is more excellent or because it is produced by the assembly of others, it is called the house [of children]. ¶ The **sixth** is Mercury, because as he is of a narrower circle and closer to the Sun and Moon, he more often moves retrograde and is more frequently "burned" <note><term>combust</term>: an astrological term for a planet being too close to the sun, weakening its power</note> by sickness and weakness; and he is allotted that kind of leadership of inconveniences, so that by frequent coming and going he performs the duty of labor and even of servitude. Therefore, as the order compelled, you look to the sixth, which obtained the name of sickness, which was more serious. ¶ The **seventh** is the Moon, when in conjunction with the Sun she conceives in the manner of a woman; in opposition, as if she were bringing forth mature births, she receives the leadership of the wife and of opposition; and since this series was joined to the seventh, it is called by the name of the wife. ¶ Likewise, with the order repeated and the return made to **Saturn**, since his nature is deadly, which death, anguishes of pains follow. Moreover, the eighth, tending toward the setting <note>the West</note>, into the same duty. The order compelled it to rightly claim the name of death. ¶ Toward [the ninth] there are altogether twin fortunes: one namely of the present, the other of the consequence, and much reason: that of Jupiter is even greater than the fortune of Venus. Venus indeed designates the happiness of this world, but Jupiter that of the [next].
